\fn{04-transcendence-gaps-source_audit.md} & \fn{source_audit.md}\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The program uses only the Python standard library and exact arithmetic;
there are no floating-point near-zero tests, and it does not use
\texttt{assert}, so its checks also run under \texttt{python -O}. It checks
finite base-digit recodings, factorial residues and idempotent Chinese
remainders, arithmetic of finite rational Laurent tails and constant terms,
the quotient-divisibility identities used for $\Asc(k,\Q)$, and exact
rational Newton iterates for a $7$-adic root of $X^2-2$. Its recorded run
returns \texttt{PASS}:
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.72\textwidth}r@{}}
\toprule
Check family & Count\\
\midrule
Base-$p$ digit words, $p=2,3,5,7$, lengths $0$--$5$ (exhaustive) & 23,941\\
Prefix-residue comparisons & 139,168\\
Recodings with an uncontrolled tail & 23,941\\
Exact finite Laurent-tail identities (1,000 seeded pairs, seed 20261003) & 38,000\\
Exact Newton stages for a $7$-adic root of $X^2-2$ & 7\\
Factorial-residue samples & 720\\
Decoded factorial-residue checks & 4,320\\
Finite coherence comparisons & 15,120\\
Idempotents modulo $8!$ & 16\\
Prime-coordinate recovery checks & 64\\
Boolean-operation identities & 272\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
These are regression checks of finite identities appearing in the proofs.
They cannot certify algebraic independence over an uncountable field,
existence of a perfect set, a Borel-model theorem, Glazer's theorem,
overspill or transfinite Hahn arithmetic; a finite check reporting
``passed'' must not be promoted into any of those assertions, and symbolic
variables in a test are not numerical transcendental witnesses. The Newton
check caps its modulus at $7^{12}$ by design, so its record shows the same
checked modulus for iterations 4--6 while the residual valuations
$16,32,64$ are checked exactly. The program writes \fn{verification.json}
beside itself unless \texttt{--output} is given; shipped in \fn{code/}, it
would write \fn{code/verification.json}, not the shipped record. At this
write it was rerun on a copy with a scratch output file (Python 3.14.4): it
passed in a few seconds, and its JSON content was identical to the shipped
record. Source~04's delivered checksum manifest \fn{SHA256SUMS} (seven
files) was verified at placement and dropped, as repository policy
requires.

\subsection{Relationship to the Surreal repository}
The repository review examined the public README, the introductory
coverage and dependency ledger, and targeted searches for overlap
\cite{Repo}. Those materials describe extensive sign-sequence, canonical
cut, normal-form, Hahn-summation, and algebraic infrastructure. They also
explicitly distinguish source manuscripts from checked Lean declarations.
The present argument is designed to fit that distinction.

The review took place while the repository was changing. The initially
inspected README and ledger had blob hashes
\begin{center}\small\ttfamily
391afd7c64cebd48b47fbe43dae26c27e8ee8047\\
6a89497cb1de3e5c7a346201f7fc006d85082426.
\end{center}
A later metadata read reported commit
\begin{center}\small\ttfamily
173eb522fdf5d4bf201c097f76b38273eb4f95e1.
\end{center}
The initially returned recursive-tree hash is not used as a commit
identifier. This manuscript does not claim to have reviewed every
repository article or every companion archive, and no repository result
is silently promoted from ``pending'' to ``proved.''
The manuscript has no pinned commit. The commit \texttt{173eb52} above
is an ancestor of the commit \texttt{c6359e4} that placed the manuscript
in the collection. The manuscript makes no claim about the content of
any report, so no statement of it had to be corrected against the
collection as it now stands.

A sound implementation would first formalize the following mathematical
interfaces; the names are proposed work units, not declarations claimed
to exist:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Cone root relocation.} Define the three-way sign split,
      the inverse, the order equivalence, and
      \eqref{isg:eq:predidentity} for ordinal-length sequences.
\item \emph{Hahn reindexing with one coefficient multiplier.} Prove
      support transport, real-linearity, order preservation, truncation
      compatibility, and strong-sum interchange.
\item \emph{Concrete initial-subgroup criterion.} Supply a checked
      version of \cref{isg:fact:EK}, including both its coefficient and
      right-ancestor conditions, before claiming the final theorem.
\item \emph{Normalization and exact inverse.} Instantiate the criterion
      for the transformed groups; then derive the spine classification,
      quotient realization, and complex-coordinate extension.
\end{enumerate}

The largest imported proof obligation is the third item. A checked
conditional theorem using that criterion would be valuable, but would
not by itself constitute a checked theorem about every actual initial
subgroup of $\No$. This package supplies no Lean file with that
obligation hidden as an axiom or an unfinished proof.

\emph{Source~02.} Its repository review was made at commit
\begin{center}\small\ttfamily
9693b28c24e6fcb317ce47969a40185c5dfef402,
\end{center}
and included the main README, the formalization ledger, the article
catalogue exposed there, and the guide of the omnific groups and lattices
report; these distinguish mathematical drafts from Lean results. It did
not constitute a claim-by-claim audit of the repository or a local Lean
build. Source~02 read source~01 from the project library, including its
quotient, suspension and relative splitting arguments, and recorded that
its location in the inspected Git tree was not established, citing it as
a preceding project draft without assigning it a repository path. That
was correct at its pin: \texttt{9693b28} is an ancestor of the commit
\texttt{c6359e4} that placed source~01, and the preceding draft is now
\cref{isg:sec:intro,isg:sec:background,isg:sec:bottom,isg:sec:surgery,isg:sec:transport,isg:sec:normalization,isg:sec:classification,isg:sec:quotient,isg:sec:complex,isg:sec:examples}
of this report (its ``Section~8'' is \cref{isg:sec:quotient}). No theorem
of source~02 is called Lean-verified. A sensible implementation order
would formalize the finite and transfinite sign-tree compression first,
followed by the support projection lemmas and the transfer of the initial
Hahn criterion. The residue and factorial-presentation arguments can be
formalized in ordinary abelian-group and ordered-group libraries
independently of a full implementation of omnific integers. This is a
dependency plan, not delivered formal code.

\emph{Source~03.}\label{isg:sc:sec:relationLean} Its repository review was
made at the ProveIt commit
\begin{center}\small\ttfamily
883e0b3b22263bd9d92fdbbf10376ca4b652b862,
\end{center}
an ancestor of its placement commit \texttt{0bd0e5527}. It consulted the
root and surreal-number reader's guides and the Lean file
\fn{Algebra/SurrealNumbers/Surreal/Foundations/OmnificResidues.lean}
(blob \texttt{aacd3cac}, unchanged at this write), naming the
declarations \fn{omnific_int_dvd_iff}, \fn{omnific_prime_pow_dvd_iff},
\fn{omnific_dvd_all_pos_int_iff} and \fn{iInf_omnific_int_multiples}
(namespace \fn{Surreal.Foundations.SignSequence}); all four exist. They
formalize, for the actual omnific ring, that divisibility by a nonzero
ordinary integer is read off the constant term and that the intersection
of the ideals $n\Oz$ is the purely infinite ideal $\Pi$: the $\Oz$ case of
standard division and of \eqref{isg:sc:eq:kernel}, which is
\replabel{odg:thm:finitequotients} and \replabel{odg:eq:divkernel}. The
constant term is the ring homomorphism \fn{omnificConstantCoeff} of
\fn{OmnificIntegers.lean}, and its uniqueness among unital ring
homomorphisms to $\Z$ is \fn{omnific_int_hom_eq_constant}
(\cref{isg:sc:sec:correction}). Nothing else of source~03 is formalized.
It makes no claim about the content of any report, except that the
Diophantine-retraction observation of \cref{isg:sc:sec:diophantine}
underlies related claims in the repository, which is true
(\replabel{odg:cor:H10}). It was not a validation of every research claim
in the repository.

Source~03 proposes a Lean plan; the names are proposed work units, not
declarations claimed to exist. The elementary layer can be formalized
independently of surreal numbers: a linear ordered commutative ring with
least positive element $1$, a predicate expressing $\ED_n$, and an
additive homomorphism $\chi:R\to\Z$ carrying $1$ to $1$; then
finite-interval rigidity, residue detection and the finite odd-factor
certificate, with the semantic predicates $\PowDy$ and $\StdDy$ kept
separate from their encoded first-order formulas, identifying their
meanings by an explicit bridge theorem. The next layer proves successor
closure and a semantic contradiction with the one bounded induction
instance; a model-theoretic interface then supplies standard division from
$\Idelta$ and forms the difference ring. Only after these reusable lemmas
should the actual omnific constant-term map be supplied, where the
declarations above already exist. For the compiler, one formalizes
capture-avoiding fresh-variable generation, substitution, local gate
uniqueness and structural truth preservation; a theorem merely stating
correctness of an abstract translator is not enough, it should identify the
executable translator and prove its bound and degree invariants. The
package includes no Lean file and makes no claim that these theorems have
been kernel-checked. In the repository's arithmetic syntax, a nonstandard
model would be a \fn{SetTheory.PA.PreModel} satisfying the relevant axioms,
not a \fn{SetTheory.PA.Model}, whose induction for every Lean predicate
makes it standard
(\fn{Logic/Interpretability/PAHF/Lean/PAHF/PASyntax.lean}); the Presburger
development \fn{Logic/PresburgerArithmetic} decides additive arithmetic
over the ordinary integers. Neither formalizes any statement of source~03.
\textup{[}This paragraph's last three sentences are the write's.\textup{]}

\emph{Source~04.}\label{isg:ptg:sec:relationLean} Its repository review was
made at the ProveIt commit
\begin{center}\small\ttfamily
7d9b8b957fa6a1cc28636ac8fce3cfaa6d230031,
\end{center}
a merge commit and an ancestor of its placement commit \texttt{12076b2e8},
through a GitHub connector. It inspected
\fn{Algebra/SurrealNumbers/README.md} and
\fn{Logic/PeanoArithmetic/ListCoding/README.md}, both unchanged at this
write, and the root README and directory metadata for navigation; it did
not review all reports or formal source files, and no Lean or Rocq build or
kernel audit was run for it. Its two claims about these files are true: the
surreal guide documents normal forms, omnific constant extraction, finite
quotients, the $p$-adic completion and its canonical image, and it
separates formalized foundations from unrefereed reports; the list-coding
guide distinguishes the standard-natural interpretation of its formulas from
internally derived universal theorems. \textup{[}Added at this write: the
$\Oz$ facts named there are Lean theorems, not only documentation; the
declarations corresponding to \cref{isg:ptg:prop:tail,isg:ptg:thm:completions,isg:ptg:thm:omnific}
are named at those statements. No statement of source~04, and not the
\textup{[write~04]} corollary, is formalized. As for source~03, a
nonstandard model of arithmetic in the repository's syntax would be a
\fn{SetTheory.PA.PreModel}, not a \fn{SetTheory.PA.Model}.\textup{]}

Source~04's formalization plan, as it gives it. The list-coding distinction
prevents a false formalization claim: a Lean proof about evaluating a
formula in $\N$ does not, by itself, justify using its totality at a
nonstandard $N\in M$. The exact-residue theorem requires a syntactic $\PA$
derivation, or a separate semantic theorem valid in every $\PA$ model, for
the bounded digit-recoding and finite-sum operations. This additional
obligation is recorded rather than silently imported from the
standard-model coding documentation.
\begingroup\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.22\textwidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.34\textwidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.38\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
Layer & Mathematical interface & Verification obligation \\
\midrule
\endhead
Arithmetic traces & Parameter-definable traces and standard bits & $\PA$ finite comprehension; deterministic oracle runs; standard-system Turing closure. \\
Descriptive coding & Analytic $\mathcal S$ and analytic $\mathcal K_F(\mathcal S)$ & Borel remainder maps; represented-space computation; analytic projection lemmas. \\
Local root closure & Individual real and $p$-adic roots & Nonuniform isolating data; effective sign bisection; quantitative Newton estimates. \\
Derivations & Countable-basis differential extension & Extension through separable algebraic fields; exact analytic graph characterization. \\
Category and measure & Aggregate dependence relations & Completed Haar measures, Fubini, Kuratowski--Ulam, and Mycielski. \\
Residue bridge & $\res_p(R_M)=\mathcal K_p(\mathcal S)\cap\Zp$ & Nonstandard digit recoding; inverse-limit compatibility; both inclusions of the image. \\
Hahn transfer & Coefficient extraction of finite relations & \Cref{isg:ptg:thm:hahn}; scalar substitution by invertible monomials. \\
Omnific application & Normal forms and constant extraction & Embedding the rational Hahn slice; comparison of topologies; finite quotient maps. \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\endgroup
The most economical first formal target is \cref{isg:ptg:thm:hahn}. It has
no descriptive set theory, no arithmetic-model coding, and no proper-class
quantification. After that, \cref{isg:ptg:prop:tail} and the same-scale
application provide a small end-to-end algebraic certificate. The
analytic-derivation lemma is a second, independent high-value target. Its
graph equivalence \eqref{isg:ptg:eq:witness1}--\eqref{isg:ptg:eq:witness3}
should be formalized before any measurable-function wrapper is attached to
it. No Lean file is included merely to give an impression of machine
verification. Source~04's proofs were developed and reviewed at the
mathematical level, and the repository was not built for it.

\subsection{Novelty and literature status}
The new contributions claimed provisionally here are the explicit
root-relocation map together with its exact predecessor identity, its
use in omnific normalization, and the sharp dyadic-spine image
classification. The extremal, quotient, and surcomplex statements are
derived refinements of that construction. The known two-level example,
the general initial-Hahn-subgroup characterization, and the classification
of initial real coefficient groups are not claimed as new.

The literature check recovered the stated question in the original
preprint, thesis, and workshop account \cite{EK,Kaplan,Workshop}, and
examined adjacent sign-concatenation work \cite{BagHoeven}. Targeted
searches did not locate a prior resolution or the specific root-relocation
argument. That negative search result is limited evidence: it does not
exclude an unindexed paper, unpublished proof, or a reformulation already
known to a specialist. Neither mathematical correctness nor priority is
certified by the absence of a search result.

\emph{Source~02.} The convex-factor theorem and its surreal applications
are proposed contributions with complete proofs; neither priority nor
independent referee certification is claimed. The Ehrlich--Kaplan
criterion, Ehrlich's divisible embedding, Je\v r\'abek's residue theory
and residue-injectivity rigidity mechanism \cite{Jerabek}, and
$\Ext(\Q,\Z)\cong\Zhat/\Z$ \cite{Strickland} are classical and credited;
no new $\Ext$ computation is claimed, and the existence of rigid
Presburger models is not claimed as new. Bagayoko and van der Hoeven's
surreal substructures \cite{BagHoeven} are a related viewpoint, not a
dependency, and \cite{EnayatHamkinsWcislo} is background on internal
arithmetic only. A targeted source review did not find a prior
formulation of the main convex-factor theorem; the search was not
exhaustive.

\emph{Source~03.} Its abstract claims ``the explicit dyadic refinement
and its assembled consequences, not a certified solution of a previously
open major conjecture''. Standard-cut definability and the failure of
induction beyond open induction in $\Oz$ are credited to Je\v r\'abek
\cite{JerabekMO} and to Enayat, \L e\l yk and Visser \cite{ELV}; Phillips
\cite{Phillips} is cited for historical context only. This report narrows
the claim further. Theorem~2.9 of \cite{ELV} already gives a
parameter-free $\Delta_0$ definition of $\N$ in every ordered Dorroh ring,
which covers $\Oz$ (witnessing ideal $\Pi$) and every ring of
\cref{isg:sc:thm:dyadic} (\cref{isg:sc:rem:ELV}); a failed bounded
induction instance in $\Oz$ follows from it at once. Bounded
definability of the standard cut in $\Oz$, and with it a failed bounded
induction instance there, are therefore not new. What remains proposed as
new is: the dyadic formula $\StdDy$ itself and its finite odd-factor
certificate; its inductiveness under $\ED_2$ alone, with the
single-instance boundary and the sharpness examples; the character results
(\cref{isg:sc:thm:characters,isg:sc:prop:diagonal,isg:sc:thm:nochar} and
their corollaries, \cref{isg:sc:cor:noadd,isg:sc:cor:nonunital,isg:sc:cor:profiles,isg:sc:cor:omnific});
the single-bound quadratic compiler with the complexity theorem and its
corollary; and the correction of \cite[Lemma~2.7]{ELV} with its repair.
\Cref{isg:sc:rem:ELV} shows that the no-character theorem can also be
reached through the four-square formula once the character classification
is available. Source~03's own literature search did not establish priority
for any exact formulation, and the \textup{[write]} statements of
\cref{isg:sc:sec:fragment} add no claim of priority.

\emph{Source~04.} Its claimed contributions are the countable-parameter
derivation argument (\cref{isg:ptg:lem:derivation}), the assembled
perfect-gap theorem, the exact standard-system/residue identifications,
including the profinite idempotents, and their coefficient-preserving Hahn
and congruence applications. It credits Fatalini and Schindler's
attribution to Ben de Bondt and Glazer as a close conceptual precursor
\cite[Section~2]{FataliniSchindler}, Ye, Yu and Zhao's
\cite[Proposition~2.6]{YeYuZhao} as nearby work on analytic subfields of
$\R$ and $\Qp$, Edgar and Miller for the dimension corollary, and Mycielski
for perfect independent sets; it establishes no priority, for the
assembled theorem or for its components. This report narrows the claim
further. \Cref{isg:ptg:cor:nomap} is weaker than
\cref{isg:sc:thm:nochar,isg:sc:cor:noadd,isg:sc:cor:omnific} of source~03;
\cref{isg:ptg:thm:hahn} follows at once from
\replabel{isc:cp:lem:coeffld}; \cref{isg:ptg:prop:tail} and the
$\Asc$-half of \cref{isg:ptg:thm:completions} are set-sized versions of
statements proved in Lean for $\Oz$ (\replabel{odg:thm:finitequotients},
\replabel{odg:eq:profinite}); and that the canonical image, not the
completion, separates the rings is said after \cref{isg:sc:prop:diagonal}.
What remains proposed as new, within this collection, is
\cref{isg:ptg:lem:derivation} with \cref{isg:ptg:prop:hulls} (whose
relation to \cite[Proposition~2.6]{YeYuZhao} was not compared at this
write), the perfect-gap and simultaneous-place theorems, the scalar fields
$\mathcal K_F(\SSy(M))$ with their faithfulness and elementarity, the exact
residue images (\cref{isg:ptg:thm:residue,isg:ptg:thm:profinite}) and the
idempotent reconstruction, the comparison of
\cref{isg:ptg:thm:completions} as a statement about Peano rings, and the
same-scale omnific family. The \textup{[write~04]} corollary adds no claim
of priority; its combinatorial content is Scott's theorem.

\subsection{Provenance, placement review, and place in the collection}
\label{isg:sec:provenance}

\paragraph{Four sources.} This report is built from four manuscripts;
sources~01 and~02 were merged in batch~29, source~03 was added in
batch~86, and source~04 in batch~90.

\emph{Source~01} is \emph{Discrete Initial Groups and Omnific
Normalization} (24 pages, 23~September 2026), manuscript~09 of the
collection's batch~28. It was delivered with a README, a source audit,
the finite checks \texttt{verify.py}, their recorded result and a build
script. It was placed in commit \texttt{c6359e4} as this directory and
written in \texttt{3d9dbe9}. Its LaTeX source is the basis of

\fn{source_audit.md}, the program and build script as \fn{code/verify.py}
and \fn{code/build.sh}, and the record as

shipped. It has no pinned repository commit; its read identifiers are
those recorded above. It contributes
\cref{isg:sec:intro,isg:sec:background,isg:sec:bottom,isg:sec:surgery,isg:sec:transport,isg:sec:normalization,isg:sec:classification,isg:sec:quotient,isg:sec:complex,isg:sec:examples},
Appendix~\ref{isg:app:compact}, Questions
\ref{isg:q:convex}--\ref{isg:q:formal}, non-claims

\emph{Source~02} is \emph{Convex Factors and Profinite Obstructions for

under the prefix \fn{02-convex-factors-}: the provenance record is shipped

\emph{Source~03} is \emph{Standard Integers in a Single Infinite
Interval: Residue Characters, Bounded Induction, and Omnific Arithmetic}

\emph{Source~04} is \emph{Perfect Transcendence Gaps in Borel Arithmetic:
