\paragraph{How the merge was made (source~04).} Source~04 was placed as an
addition because its exact profinite image answers \cref{isg:sc:q:images},
a question this report names, for nonstandard models of Peano arithmetic;
the question asks about $\Idelta$, so it is answered in part and re-scoped
in a dated note after its statement. Source~04 does not cite this report,
so its relation to the report is stated by the write: the paragraph
``Relation to this report and its neighbours'' of
\cref{isg:ptg:sec:overview}, the bracketed comparisons after
\cref{isg:ptg:fact:glazer}, \eqref{isg:ptg:eq:res},
\cref{isg:ptg:prop:tail,isg:ptg:thm:completions,isg:ptg:cor:nomap,isg:ptg:thm:hahn,isg:ptg:thm:omnific}
and in \cref{isg:sec:audit}, the subsection \cref{isg:ptg:sec:images} with
the corollary \cref{isg:ptg:cor:images}, marked \textup{[write~04]}, and
(N56). The alternatives considered at placement were a part of the report
\nolinkurl{docs/foundations-and-computation/polish-models-of-omnific-arithmetic},
which studies Borel and Polish models of arithmetic motivated by Glazer
but names no question that source~04 answers, and a new report; the
perfect-gap material, the bulk of source~04, travels with it. Source~04's
material is printed as it stands, in four new sections inserted after
\cref{isg:sc:sec:compiler}; the former Sections~23--25 (verification,
questions, conclusion) moved down by four, so the questions numbered 24.$x$
at the write of source~03 are now 28.$x$, and no theorem-like number of
Sections~1--22 or of the appendices changed. Its symbols are renamed as in
\cref{isg:ptg:sec:conventions}, which also gives the correspondence of its
numbering, because its shipped ledger and audit use its own. Its
introduction is \cref{isg:ptg:sec:intro,isg:ptg:sec:overview}; its
dependency ledger, executable checks, formalization plan and novelty
material are source-04 paragraphs of \cref{isg:sec:audit}; its eleven
questions follow source~03's in \cref{isg:sec:further}; its limitations are
(N42)--(N55); its conclusion is a paragraph of \cref{isg:sec:conclusion}.
The checklist of Appendix~\ref{isg:app:checklist} gained six rows. No
mathematical statement, proof or example of sources~01--03 changed, and no
label was renamed or removed.

\paragraph{How the merge was made (source~03).} Source~03 was placed as an
addition because, with \cref{isg:cf:main:presburger}, it answers part of
\cref{isg:cf:q:fragment}, a question this report names; it does not cite
this report, so the combination is stated here, marked \textup{[write]}
(\cref{isg:sc:def:Tdy,isg:sc:cor:fragment,isg:sc:prop:iopen},
\cref{isg:sc:rem:fragment}), and the question is re-scoped in a dated note
after its statement. The alternative considered at placement was a part of
the report
\nolinkurl{docs/foundations-and-computation/polish-models-of-omnific-arithmetic},
which collects the Glazer-motivated manuscripts of the same delivery; it
names no question that source~03 answers. Source~03's material is printed
as it stands, in four new sections inserted after \cref{isg:cf:sec:boundaries};
the former Sections~19--21 (verification, questions, conclusion) moved
down by four, and no theorem-like number of Sections~1--18 or of the
appendices changed. Its symbols are renamed as in
\cref{isg:sc:sec:conventions}, notably $\mathsf P,\mathsf S$ to
$\PowDy,\StdDy$, which keeps them apart from the Diophantine report's
existential $\operatorname{Std}$ (\replabel{odg:thm:standarddef}). Its
introduction is \cref{isg:sc:sec:intro,isg:sc:sec:overview}; its
verification, Lean-plan and novelty material is in the source-03
paragraphs of \cref{isg:sec:audit}, its questions follow source~02's in
\cref{isg:sec:further}, its limitations are (N30)--(N41), and its
conclusion is a paragraph of \cref{isg:sec:conclusion}. Added at this
write, beyond the \textup{[write]} statements: \cref{isg:sc:rem:ELV}, the
comparisons with \cref{isg:cf:prop:residuesplit} and with the Lean theorem
\fn{omnific_int_hom_eq_constant}, the narrower novelty statement, the
pointer to the batch-86 manuscript~06, and the repository paragraph's last
three sentences; each says so. The checklist of
Appendix~\ref{isg:app:checklist} gained six rows, five from source~03's
hypotheses section and one for the \textup{[write]} corollary. Source~02's text received three bracketed
pointers (after \cref{isg:cf:thm:PA}, after \cref{isg:cf:cor:unit} and in
\textup{(N26)}). No mathematical statement, proof or example of sources~01
or~02 changed, and no label was renamed or removed.

\paragraph{How the merge was made (source~02).} Source~02 was placed as an
addition because it answers two questions this report named,
\cref{isg:q:convex,isg:q:limit}. Its material is printed as it stands,
with these choices. Results that both sources contain are printed once:
the Ehrlich--Kaplan criterion (\cref{isg:fact:EK}), the classification of
initial real groups (\cref{isg:fact:real}), the caution against Hahn
completion (\cref{isg:rem:nocompletion,isg:cf:rem:membership}), the
integer-bottom suspension (\cref{isg:thm:suspension}; source~02 re-proves
it from the criterion, by the same argument), the description of $\Oz$
(\eqref{isg:eq:Oz}) and the rectangular convention
(\cref{isg:def:rectangular}). Source~02's foundations section is replaced
by \cref{isg:sec:background} and \cref{isg:cf:sec:conventions}; its
symbols that collide with source~01's are renamed
(\cref{isg:cf:sec:conventions}); its Theorems A--C are Theorems D--F. Its
verification, repository, novelty, question and non-claim material is
merged into the corresponding parts of
\cref{isg:sec:audit,isg:sec:further}; its notation table is replaced by
the renaming table of \cref{isg:cf:sec:conventions} and the definitions
at their places, its dependency summary is in \cref{isg:sec:audit}, and
its independent-review checks are in
Appendix~\ref{isg:app:checklist}. Three statements are added in the merge, each
with a complete proof and marked \textup{[merge]}:
\cref{isg:cf:prop:cyclic} (source~02's construction for $\Z\varepsilon$
is source~01's $\rho\circ\tht$), \cref{isg:cf:cor:limit} (limit stages of
the iteration of \cref{isg:cor:iteration}) and \cref{isg:cf:cor:periods}
(period groups of the trigonometry report); \cref{isg:cf:rem:formulations}
records how the two versions of Ehrlich and Kaplan's optimality theorem
differ. The Ehrlich--Kaplan numbering table of \cref{isg:sec:intro} gained
the two rows for Section~8. No mathematical statement, proof or example
of source~01 was changed. Where source~01 said that convex quotients and
limit stages were not covered (after
\cref{isg:cor:iteration,isg:cor:relative}, in
\cref{isg:q:convex,isg:q:limit} and in \textup{(N10)}), the text now says
which source proves what; the title page, abstract, status box,
conventions, this section and the conclusion mention the second source.

\paragraph{What the write changed (source~01).} Every label now carries the prefix
\texttt{isg:}. The journal numbering of the Ehrlich--Kaplan results
(\cref{isg:sec:intro}) and the wording of the journal version of their
criterion (after \cref{isg:fact:EK}) were added, as were
\cref{isg:sec:conventions}, this subsection, the shipped file names, a
definition of $\ct$ (which the manuscript used without defining it),
labels on formerly unlabelled statements, and the collected list of
non-claims (\cref{isg:sec:nonclaims}). Cross-references now name each kind
of statement correctly; in the delivered source every reference to a
lemma, proposition, corollary, remark or imported fact printed as
``theorem''. No mathematical statement, proof or example was changed.

\paragraph{Placement review (source~01).} When source~01 was placed, every step
of the argument was checked by hand: the bottom-coefficient structure,
both cases of the predecessor identity \eqref{isg:eq:predidentity} and its
failure at the old minimum, the ambient transport, every dyadic obligation
in the proof of \cref{isg:thm:A}, both directions of \cref{isg:thm:B}
with its two counterexamples, the extremal groups, the size and depth
corollaries, the splitting and suspension theorems, and every example of
\cref{isg:sec:examples}. No gap was found. The Ehrlich--Kaplan criterion
was checked as a statement, against the arXiv text and the author-hosted
text of the journal version: \cref{isg:fact:EK} is a faithful reading of
\cite[Theorem~1 and its proof]{EK}, equivalently of Theorem~5.1 of
\cite{EKjournal}, and \cref{isg:fact:real} is \cite[Lemma~3]{EK}. The
criterion was not re-proved; the whole argument rests on it. This review
is not a refereeing, and the solution proposed here has not been refereed.
Its scope is source~01's material; it does not cover
\cref{isg:cf:sec:cuts,isg:cf:sec:compression,isg:cf:sec:factors,isg:cf:sec:presburger,isg:cf:sec:factorial,isg:cf:sec:modeltheory,isg:cf:sec:complex,isg:cf:sec:boundaries}.

\paragraph{Placement review (source~02).} When source~02 was placed, its
proofs were checked by hand and no error was found: the exponent-cut
lemmas and the canonical splitting, the compression theorem with its
uniqueness and nested coherence, the transfer of every dyadic obligation
in \cref{isg:cf:thm:transport} (coefficient groups are unchanged and
every target right ancestor comes from a source one by
\eqref{isg:cf:eq:rightreflect}), the residue lemmas and
\cref{isg:cf:prop:residuesplit}, the factorial family (its table, the
transitions, $n!x_n=x_1+S_ne$, $S_n\le2(n-1)!$ and the finite
retractions), the extension classes and rigidity, the dense example
(initiality of $W$: its exponents $0,1,2$ have no right ancestors in the
class, and $\Q$ and $\Z$ are initial in $\R$), the omitted type, the
ultrapower and Peano arguments, the omnific residues and the rectangular
transport. The compression was re-implemented independently and tested
on all 496 nonempty convex intervals of the sign tree of lengths at most
four (order, injectivity, initial image, prefix and right-ancestor
reflection) and for nested coherence on the tree of lengths at most
three. The elementary equivalence in \cref{isg:cf:thm:dense} rests on a
standard two-sorted interpretation, checked as an argument. The
Ehrlich--Kaplan criterion and Ehrlich's embedding theorem were not
re-proved. For the merge statements, \eqref{isg:cf:eq:cyclic} was
confirmed by a finite computation for $\alpha\le3$ and words of length at
most six, and the iteration in \cref{isg:cf:cor:limit} for three removal
steps on those trees; that computation is not shipped. This review is not
a refereeing, and it does not extend to source~01's material beyond the
consistency statements of \cref{isg:cf:sec:cyclic,isg:cf:sec:limit}.

\paragraph{Placement review (source~03).} At this write, source~03's
proofs were checked by hand and no error was found: both cases
of the odd-factor certificate (character zero, and character $2^km_0$ with
$m_0$ odd and possibly negative) with the witness bounds, the doubling and
successor closure under $\ED_2$, the single-instance boundary, both
sharpness rings, the character classification and the diagonal-image
criterion, standard division in $\Idelta$, the vanishing theorem and its
corollaries, the explicit non-diagonal profiles (including the passage from
residues modulo $2^{k+j}$ to residues of $y/2^k$), the counterexample and
the repair, the examples $\Asc_0$ (with the three certificates of
\cref{isg:sc:ex:certificates} recomputed), $\Asc(k,\Gamma)$ and $\Oz$, the
equal-bound guards, the compiler and the complexity reductions. The
open-induction theorem for $\Asc_{\mathrm{Sh}}$ and the effective
arithmetical definition of a computable presentation were checked as
imported statements, not re-proved. Lemma~2.7 and Theorem~2.9 of
\cite{ELV} were read at placement and again at this write in the v2 PDF
(pages~11--12): Lemma~2.7 is printed as ``The witnessing ideal of a
Dorroh ring is unique'', its proof makes the step that
\cref{isg:sc:prop:correction} refutes, and Theorem~2.9 states that the
natural numbers are $\Delta_0$-definable in the nonnegative part of every
ordered Dorroh ring. The \textup{[write]} statements were proved at this
write. This review is not a refereeing.

\paragraph{Placement review (source~04).} At this write, source~04's proofs
were read in full and checked by hand, and no error was found: the trace and
Turing-ideal lemmas, the analyticity of $b_M$ and of the scalar fields, the
size gap, faithfulness of the digit encodings, the real bisection and the
$p$-adic Newton estimates of \cref{isg:ptg:lem:roots}, both elementarity
arguments (the $p$-adic one through local constancy of power classes), the
derivation lemma (the witness conditions
\eqref{isg:ptg:eq:witness1}--\eqref{isg:ptg:eq:witness3}, uniqueness of the
value, analyticity of the graph without enumerating $k$, and the passage
from analytic graph to Borel to continuous), properness of the hulls, the
Fubini and Kuratowski--Ulam induction, the product argument for all places
(injectivity from the relation $X_1-X_2=0$), both inclusions of the
$p$-adic and profinite images with the overspill step, the idempotents, the
strict inclusion and the finite quotients of $\Zhat(\mathcal S)$, the
residue tower of $\Asc(k,\Q)$, the comparison and the nonidentification,
the Hahn transfer, the omnific family and its discreteness. Glazer's
theorem, Mycielski's theorem in the form of \cite[Theorem~1]{DolezalKubis},
Macintyre's quantifier elimination, Edgar and Miller's theorem and the
classical descriptive set theory were taken as imported and not re-proved;
Scott's theorem, used by the \textup{[write~04]} corollary, likewise. This
review is not a refereeing.

\paragraph{Literature.} The placement review of source~01 ran targeted web
searches (``discrete initial subgroup'', ``initial subgroup'' together
with ``omnific integers'', and the title of \cite{EK}). They found only
the Ehrlich--Kaplan paper, Kaplan's thesis and unrelated pages, and no
resolution of the question. The search was targeted, not exhaustive. The
citations of Kaplan's thesis (its Sections~6.2 and~7.1) and of the
Oberwolfach report (printed pages 3355--3356) are the manuscript's; they
were not re-checked at placement. For source~02 no separate literature
search is recorded at placement; its own review was targeted and found no
matching formulation of the convex-factor theorem. Its citations of
Je\v r\'abek, Strickland, Enayat--Hamkins--Wcis\l o and Bagayoko--van der
Hoeven are the manuscript's and were not re-checked. Its account of
Ehrlich and Kaplan's Section~8 was compared with the arXiv text and the
authors' text of the journal version at this write
(\cref{isg:cf:rem:formulations}).
For source~03, the arXiv records of \cite{ELV} (versions of 20~August
and 30~September 2025) and of \cite{Raffer} were checked at this write,
and the cited pages of \cite{ELV} were read
(\cref{isg:sec:provenance}, placement review); the citations of Glazer
\cite{Glazer} were compared with the arXiv v1 text, whose Theorem~2,
Corollary~1 and Questions~1--2 say what is attributed to them here.
Je\v r\'abek's MathOverflow answer \cite{JerabekMO}, Phillips's article
\cite{Phillips} and Shepherdson's paper \cite{Shepherdson} were not
re-checked; source~03 itself consulted Phillips only through the
publisher's extract and Shepherdson only through \cite{Raffer}.
For source~04, none of its citations was re-checked at this write: the
seminar announcement and slides of \cite{GlazerSS}, the versions of
\cite{FataliniSchindler,YeYuZhao,EdgarMiller,DolezalKubis,BKMM} and
\cite{Mycielski,Macintyre,Kechris} are as source~04 records them in its
shipped source audit, which also says that the original Mycielski PDF was
not fetched. Its citation of Glazer's \emph{A Topological Tennenbaum
Theorem} is \cite{Glazer}, already checked for source~03.

\paragraph{Place in the collection.} At the placement of source~01, no
other report named Question~2 (Question~9.1) of Ehrlich and Kaplan, and
none treated initial subgroups: a search of the reports and of the Lean sources for
``initial subgroup'', ``discrete initial'' and ``discretely ordered
initial'' found nothing outside this directory. The paper \cite{EK} is
cited as background in two reports: the foundations report cites it among
the initial-embedding results next to its sign-tree categoricity
(\replabel{found:sub:signtree}), and the definable-surreals report for
the simplicity theory of simplest separators
(\replabel{dsn:sec:foundations}). The latter's maximal initial elementary
exponential core (\replabel{dsn:thm:core}) concerns initial
\emph{subfields} of definable surreals; it is related in spirit and
neither overlaps nor conflicts with the present groups. The omnific
integers used here are those of the Diophantine and set-sized-quotient
reports (\replabel{odg:def:rings}, \replabel{osq:eq:Ozdef}); their
discreteness is \replabel{odg:prop:units}, and the constant coefficient
is the additive map of \replabel{osq:lem:ct}. The Diophantine report and
the foundations report also cite a different Ehrlich--Kaplan paper,
\emph{Surreal ordered exponential fields}, for the initial integer part
$\Oz\cap K$ of an initial subfield $K$ (the status note after
\replabel{odg:thm:floor}, and \replabel{found:sub:canonicalexp}). That
statement concerns subrings and integer parts. The groups $H\subseteq\Oz$
here are additive subgroups and $\Rect$ is not multiplicative, so there is
no conflict. No \texttt{isg:} statement has a Lean implementation.

Source~02 adds one neighbour. The trigonometry report studies the
normalized period groups $\mathcal I(\Psi)$ of surcomplex phases, which
are $\Z$-groups for set-sized fields, with the same Je\v r\'abek residue
theory: its residue $\res_{\mathcal I}$ and maximal divisible subgroup
$\mathcal I^{\mathrm{div}}$ (\replabel{trigonometry:per:sec:defect}) are
$\res_G$ and $G^{\dv}$ here, and its profinite defect is that residue
modulo $\Z$. By \cref{isg:cf:cor:periods} a period group is initially
realizable exactly when its profinite defect vanishes. There is no
conflict: that report's initiality statements concern the kernel-multiplier
ring (\replabel{trigonometry:per:thm:robustness}), a subring, and the
initial integer part $\Oz\cap F$, not the realizability of period groups.
The divisible-kernel lemma is classical in both places. The set-sized
quotient report (\replabel{osq:eq:Ozdef}) and the Diophantine report use
the same $\Pi$ and $\ct$ as \cref{isg:cf:prop:omnificresidue}.

Related (batch 33). The exponential-relations report
(\nolinkurl{docs/foundations-and-computation/exponential-relations-over-omnific-integers})
reads the class-sized
$\Oz$ formula by formula as a $\Z$-group, and so as a model of Presburger
arithmetic in the sense of \cref{isg:cf:sec:intro}. Its division with
remainder (\replabel{exr:prop:division}) is \cref{isg:cf:lem:residues} for
$G=\Oz$, $e=1$, re-derived independently, and its lexicographic splitting
$\Oz=\Pi\oplus\Z$ is the split form of \cref{isg:cf:main:presburger} with
$G^{\dv}=\Pi$. It expands this group by the zero predicates of finite
exponential sums with rational slopes and proves the result a definitional
expansion of Presburger arithmetic, with $\Z$ an elementary substructure of
$\Oz$ (\replabel{exr:thm:rational}). Nothing there concerns initiality, and
no statement here concerns exponential predicates.

Source~03 (batch 86) adds three neighbours. The Diophantine report
defines $\Z$ in $\Oz$ by an \emph{existential} quartic formula
(\replabel{odg:thm:standarddef}) and gives failed existential induction
instances on $\Oz_{\ge0}$ (\replabel{odg:thm:induction},
\replabel{odg:rem:ringinduction}); $\StdDy$ is bounded but not
existential, so it bears on neither the Diophantine guard question
(\replabel{odg:q:guard}) nor the Diophantine definitions there. The
open-induction statements of the collection (the Shepherdson passage
after \replabel{odg:thm:induction} for $\Oz$, and
\replabel{dsn:cor:openinduction} for the definable integer part $I_A$ of
the definable-surreals report, whose constant-term retraction and
standard division are \replabel{dsn:prop:ct}) are consistent with
\cref{isg:sc:thm:boundary}: these rings satisfy open induction, have
standard division and a ring retraction onto $\Z$, and therefore fail the
bounded instance $\Ind(\StdDy)$. The uniqueness of the constant term
(\replabel{odg:prop:canonicalct}) is the $\Oz$ case of
\cref{isg:sc:prop:repair}, and the existence transfer behind Hilbert's
tenth problem over $\Oz$ (\replabel{odg:cor:H10}) is the Diophantine
retraction of \cref{isg:sc:sec:diophantine}.
Finally, the report
\nolinkurl{docs/foundations-and-computation/polish-models-of-omnific-arithmetic},
placed from the same delivery's manuscripts and being written at the same
time, studies Polish topologies on models of Presburger arithmetic and
related obstructions, motivated by the same paper of Glazer; its first
part proposes an affirmative construction for Glazer's Question~2.
Source~03 is not topological, and its obstruction precedes any topology
(\cref{isg:sc:rem:regularity}). Batch-86 manuscript~06, merged there,
proves the failed open-induction instance $x^2<t$ in $\Z+t\R[t]$, which
is the instance given for $\Asc_0$ in \cref{isg:sc:sec:rings}. No
\texttt{isg:sc:} statement has a Lean implementation.

Source~04 (batch 90) adds these neighbours. The independent-surreal-copies
report proves the coefficient-extension lemma
\replabel{isc:cp:lem:coeffld} from which \cref{isg:ptg:thm:hahn} follows,
and itself studies algebraic independence in Hahn fields;
source~04's coefficient fields $k_{\R,Y}$ are a new instance, not a new
method. The Diophantine report proves the finite quotients and the
$p$-adic and profinite completions of $\Oz$
(\replabel{odg:thm:finitequotients}, \replabel{odg:eq:profinite}), of which
\cref{isg:ptg:prop:tail} and the $\Asc$-half of
\cref{isg:ptg:thm:completions} are set-sized versions; its example of
$\omega$, $\sqrt2\,\omega$ and $0$, identical in every finite quotient, is
of the same kind as the family of \cref{isg:ptg:thm:omnific}, whose
members are moreover algebraically independent over a large coefficient
field; $\omega$ and $\sqrt2\,\omega$ are not members, since $1$ and
$\sqrt2$ are computable. The report
\nolinkurl{docs/foundations-and-computation/polish-models-of-omnific-arithmetic}
studies Borel and Polish models of arithmetic after Glazer's topological
paper; source~04 uses a different paper of Glazer's, on standard systems,
needs no topology on $R_M$ (\cref{isg:ptg:sec:limits}), and answers none
of that report's questions. The report
\nolinkurl{docs/foundations-and-computation/cantor-families-of-surreal-subfields},
written from another manuscript of the same delivery, studies Cantor
families of countable subfields of one countable surreal field; it shares
words with source~04 (Cantor families, subfields), not theorems. No
\texttt{isg:ptg:} statement has a Lean implementation.

\section{Further questions and exact boundaries}\label{isg:sec:further}

The construction establishes an additive normal form while deliberately
relinquishing several stronger kinds of compatibility. The following
questions isolate possible further work without asserting that each is
a previously published open problem.

\begin{question}[Other convex quotients; answered by source~02]\label{isg:q:convex}
Which convex subgroups $C$ of an initial group $A\subseteq\No$ admit
an explicit initial realization of $A/C$ by a normal-form transport?
\end{question}
Source~01 handles $C=\Z\varepsilon$. For a larger convex subgroup,
one would need to identify the removed exponent region and determine
whether its boundary can be relocated without creating unmet dyadic
obligations. A numerical quotient of exponent chains alone would not
settle the simplicity condition.

\emph{Status: answered, by source~02.} All of them.
\Cref{isg:cf:main:factor,isg:cf:thm:transport} realize $A/C$ explicitly:
the removed exponent region is the lower segment $\Gamma_C$
(\cref{isg:cf:lem:arch}), the quotient is represented by the upper
projection $P_{\Gamma^C}$, a difference of leading truncations
(\cref{isg:cf:lem:restriction,isg:cf:thm:split}), and compression of
$\Gamma^C$ creates no unmet dyadic obligation because it reflects right
ancestors \eqref{isg:cf:eq:rightreflect}; it is not a numerical quotient
of exponent chains (\cref{isg:cf:ex:compression}). For
$C=\Z\varepsilon$ the transport is source~01's
(\cref{isg:cf:prop:cyclic}).

\begin{question}[Limit iteration; answered by source~02]\label{isg:q:limit}
When an iterated removal of discrete bottom layers continues through
infinitely many stages, which hypotheses give a coherent initial
realization at a limit stage?
\end{question}
In source~01, finite iteration follows from \cref{isg:cor:iteration};
limit iteration requires a separate control of supports and of the
changing sign-tree presentations. No commutation with such an unproved
transfinite limit is used in source~01.

\emph{Status: answered, by source~02 with a corollary of the merge.} No
hypothesis is needed. The union of the removed layers is a convex
subgroup, whose quotient is realized directly from its exponent cut
(\cref{isg:cf:cor:closure}), coherently with every earlier stage
(\cref{isg:cf:cor:coherentfactors}); \cref{isg:cf:cor:limit} states this
for the iteration of \cref{isg:cor:iteration}. The required control is
supplied by compression, which is defined position by position and never
as a limit of successive deletions (\cref{isg:cf:rem:limit}).

\begin{question}[Multiplicative compatibility on restricted classes]\label{isg:q:multiplicative}
For which special initial groups does some normalization to \Oz also
preserve products wherever those products are part of a specified
algebraic structure?
\end{question}
The counterexample with $1\cdot1$ rules out a general claim for our
normalization. Any useful positive result must impose additional
conditions on exponent addition and coefficient rescaling, not merely
on the order of supports.

\begin{question}[Formal certification and uniqueness principles]\label{isg:q:formal}
Can the root-relocation proof and the concrete initiality criterion be
fully verified in the repository's foundational model, and what natural
extra conditions single out this normalization among the possible
ordered-group isomorphisms to initial omnific groups?
\end{question}
Our formulas are canonical relative to the given sign model, normal
forms, and least positive scale. They are not asserted to be invariant
under arbitrary abstract group isomorphisms. The uniqueness in
\cref{isg:thm:B} concerns the inverse under the fixed map, not uniqueness of
all possible initial embeddings.

The next four questions are source~02's. They mark extensions of the
proved statements rather than prerequisites left unproved in their
proofs, and they are not asserted to be previously published open
problems (the second is related to the published Question~1 / 8.1 of
Ehrlich and Kaplan, \cref{isg:cf:sec:optimality}).

\begin{question}[Intrinsic characterization]\label{isg:cf:q:intrinsic}
Which initially realizable ordered groups are characterized by intrinsic
conditions on all convex factors and the splitting of convex extensions,
without first choosing a Hahn presentation?
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Intermediate theories]\label{isg:cf:q:intermediate}
Can the nonsplit convex-extension obstruction be arranged inside a
set-sized model of every proper intermediate theory appearing in the
Ehrlich--Kaplan optimality question?
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Arithmetic fragments]\label{isg:cf:q:fragment}
What is the weakest arithmetic fragment whose nonstandard models have
ordered additive group completions excluded by the factorial residue
type? Which analogous computable residue types yield sharper bounds?
\end{question}
\emph{Note (3~October 2026, at the write of source~03): partly answered,
re-scoped.} For exclusion from $\IR$ by any argument, the threshold is now
located: the additive completion of every nonstandard model of $\Idelta$,
indeed of the theory $\Tdy$ whose only induction axiom is the one bounded
sentence $\Ind(\StdDy)$, is not initially realizable
(\cref{isg:sc:cor:fragment}), while a countable nonstandard model of open
induction with standard division has an initially realizable additive
group (\cref{isg:sc:prop:iopen}). So the threshold lies strictly above
$\Iopen$ and at or below $\Tdy\subseteq\Idelta$. Still open: the exact
weakest fragment (whether $\Tdy$ is strictly weaker than $\Idelta$ is
\cref{isg:sc:q:threshold}); and the question as literally posed, for
exclusion \emph{by the factorial type} $\tau_*$, since whether nonstandard
models of $\Idelta$ realize $\tau_*$ is not addressed. For the second
sentence, \cref{isg:sc:cor:profiles} supplies an analogous computable
residue obstruction, the elements satisfying $\PowDy$ above the standard
cut, but it is stated in the ring language, not as a type of the
ordered-group language (\cref{isg:sc:rem:fragment}).

\begin{question}[Compression and multiplication]\label{isg:cf:q:multiplicative}
Under what additional compatibility conditions does exponent-tree
compression preserve a specified multiplication or a specified action
of a coefficient domain? The unrestricted additive result provides no
such compatibility automatically.
\end{question}
This is the counterpart for compression of \cref{isg:q:multiplicative},
which asks the same for the normalization $\Rect$.

The next eight questions are source~03's. They are proposed directions
arising from its proofs, not all asserted to be previously unposed or
established open problems; a focused literature check should precede any
priority claim.

\begin{question}[Minimal detector complexity]\label{isg:sc:q:detector}
Under standard division and an integer retraction, can one choose a
uniform standard-cut formula with fewer bounded variables or fewer
alternations than $\StdDy$? Which additional algebraic assumptions permit
bounded-existential definitions? Distinguish the exact displayed detector
from the broader definability methods already available.
\end{question}
The four-square formula of \cite[Theorem~2.9]{ELV} is one such broader
method (\cref{isg:sc:rem:ELV}); the existential definitions of the
Diophantine report (\replabel{odg:thm:standarddef}) are unbounded.

\begin{question}[The induction threshold for additive rigidity]\label{isg:sc:q:threshold}
The one instance $\Ind(\StdDy)$, together with standard division, already
excludes every nonzero integer character in a nonstandard ring. Determine
which familiar weak induction theories prove enough of these assumptions,
and compare their strengths over a precisely fixed base theory.
\end{question}
In the terms of \cref{isg:sc:def:Tdy}: $\Idelta\vdash\Tdy$, and
$\Iopen+\{\ED_n^+\}\nvdash\Tdy$ (\cref{isg:sc:prop:iopen}); this question
bears directly on \cref{isg:cf:q:fragment}.

\begin{question}[Canonical residue images]\label{isg:sc:q:images}
Which subrings of $\Zhat$ occur as $\im\res_{R_M}$ for nonstandard
$M\models\Idelta$? \Cref{isg:sc:cor:profiles} proves only that this image
contains more than the diagonal integers. Investigate how induction,
saturation, and the chosen model constrain the extra residue profiles.
\end{question}
\emph{Note (4~October 2026, at the write of source~04): answered for Peano
arithmetic, re-scoped for $\Idelta$.} For every nonstandard model $M$ of
Peano arithmetic, $\im\res_{R_M}=\Zhat(\SSy(M))$, the ring of profinite
integers whose factorial residue sequences are computable from one member
of the standard system (\cref{isg:ptg:thm:profinite}); its idempotents are
exactly the $\mathbf 1_{\mathsf A}$ with $\mathsf A\in\SSy(M)$
(\cref{isg:ptg:cor:idempotents}), so the image determines the standard
system and depends on nothing else, and it contains every profinite integer
with a computable factorial residue sequence, $\zeta_*$ among them. With
Scott's theorem, the images of the countable nonstandard models of $\PA$
are exactly the $\Zhat(\mathcal S)$ for countable Scott sets $\mathcal S$
(\cref{isg:ptg:cor:images}, \textup{[write~04]}). Still open: the question
as posed, for models of $\Idelta$ that are not models of $\PA$, where the
proofs of source~04 do not apply (they use induction for arbitrary
arithmetic formulas, overspill and internal finite sums;
\cref{isg:ptg:q:weaker} asks for the weakest theory that suffices), and
the uncountable Peano case beyond the Scott-set description, which is
Scott's problem. For $\Idelta$, \cref{isg:sc:cor:profiles} remains the only
information here. \Cref{isg:ptg:q:completions} asks a related question
about completions with a distinguished image.

\begin{question}[Fixed syntactic resources]\label{isg:sc:q:resources}
Classify truth complexity when the single-bound quadratic fragment has a
fixed number of variables, a fixed alternation bound, or a fixed
gate-depth bound. \Cref{isg:sc:thm:complexity} allows these quantities to
grow with the input sentence and does not answer the fixed-resource
versions.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Weaker division hypotheses]\label{isg:sc:q:division}
Division by $2$ suffices for uniqueness of a normalized character, whereas
normalization of arbitrary characters used all standard moduli. Determine
exact character groups when division is available only for selected prime
powers, and identify the weakest assumptions supporting the no-additive-map
conclusion.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Integer parts inside surreal fields]\label{isg:sc:q:integerparts}
When a nonstandard arithmetic field embeds in $\No$, characterize the
integer parts that can be its arithmetic image. Such an image cannot lie
in $\Oz$ under the hypotheses of \cref{isg:sc:cor:omnific}; which
structural features distinguish the admissible integer parts from the
canonical omnific one?
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Regularity of residue maps]\label{isg:sc:q:regularity}
In presentations of weaker arithmetic on Polish spaces, how do continuity
or Borel measurability of residue maps and integer characters constrain
multiplication? A useful comparison with Glazer's work must state the
induction and regularity hypotheses separately, rather than infer one from
the other.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[A focused proof-assistant milestone]\label{isg:sc:q:milestone}
Formalize the source-level counterexample, the $\ED_2$ uniqueness repair,
and the dyadic certificate before attempting the full interpretation
compiler. These are short, reusable targets with explicit assumptions and
little dependence on heavy surreal or model-theoretic libraries.
\end{question}
For $\Oz$, the repair's ring-homomorphism case is already a Lean theorem
(\fn{omnific_int_hom_eq_constant}, \cref{isg:sc:sec:correction}).
Source~03 names as immediate priorities the last question and a targeted
comparison of the exact additive-character statement with older work on
nonstandard arithmetic. A separate dependency audit should identify every
use of the incorrect unrestricted uniqueness lemma in \cite{ELV} and
replace it either by a chosen witnessing ideal or by a valid additional
hypothesis.

The next eleven questions are source~04's. Except where a cited literature
problem is explicitly named, they are not asserted to be previously
published open problems. They are ordered by the clarity of the next proof
obligation rather than by an unsupported prediction of difficulty.

\begin{question}[Weaker arithmetic]\label{isg:ptg:q:weaker}
What is the weakest arithmetical theory for which its nonstandard Borel
models have the complete exact-residue and local-field package proved in
\cref{isg:ptg:sec:standard,isg:ptg:sec:perfect,isg:ptg:sec:residues,isg:ptg:sec:hahn}?
\end{question}
The specific tasks are to separate definable-trace coding, downward Turing
closure, and nonstandard finite recoding. Glazer's no-full-system input
already reaches $\EFA$, but the Peano proof uses stronger induction. A
successful weakening must replace those uses, not simply change ``$\PA$''
to ``$\EFA$'' in the statement. \textup{[}Added at this write: for the residue images, this
bears directly on \cref{isg:sc:q:images}, which asks about
$\Idelta$.\textup{]}

\begin{question}[Effective strength of the perfect-gap construction]\label{isg:ptg:q:strength}
Given codes for the analytic field and its relative-closure property, what
oracle strength is sufficient to produce a code for an independent Cantor
set? Which reverse-mathematical subsystem proves the countable-parameter
version?
\end{question}
The derivation proof and the Mycielski extraction have different logical
costs. The inability to give a computable parametrization without extra
information is already established in \cref{isg:ptg:sec:limits}; it does
not settle the optimal relative construction.

\begin{question}[Descriptive complexity of the scalar image]\label{isg:ptg:q:complexity}
Which Borel ranks or analytic non-Borel complexities occur for $\SSy(M)$,
$\mathcal K_\infty(\SSy(M))$, and $\mathcal K_p(\SSy(M))$ as $M$ varies
through Borel nonstandard $\PA$ models?
\end{question}
\Cref{isg:ptg:prop:faithful} recovers ideal inclusion from field inclusion,
but does not identify an optimal descriptive complexity. The digit
embeddings give natural lower-bound reductions that a classification should
exploit.

\begin{question}[A shared digit family at all places]\label{isg:ptg:q:digits}
Can one choose a Cantor family of binary sets $\mathsf A$ such that the
fixed encodings $c_\infty(\mathsf A)$ and $c_p(\mathsf A)$ are
simultaneously algebraically independent over all the corresponding model
fields?
\end{question}
\Cref{isg:ptg:thm:simultaneous} allows different continuous coordinate
maps. It does not answer this version. Pullback of a meager relation by an
arbitrary continuous embedding need not be meager, so the earlier product
argument cannot simply be reused on the diagonal digit image.

\begin{question}[$p$-adic dimension]\label{isg:ptg:q:dimension}
What are the possible Hausdorff and packing dimensions of proper analytic
relatively algebraically closed subfields of $\Qp$, especially those arising
from standard systems?
\end{question}
The proof establishes Haar-nullness and meagerness. It does not show
$p$-adic dimension zero. A comparison with the real ring-dimension
phenomena studied by Edgar--Miller and Ye--Yu--Zhao is a concrete starting
point, not a license to transfer their real conclusion.

\begin{question}[Realization of local shadows]\label{isg:ptg:q:realization}
Which proper analytic subfields $k\preccurlyeq\Qp$ are of the form
$\mathcal K_p(\SSy(M))$ for a Borel nonstandard $\PA$ model? Which
compatible collections of real and $p$-adic fields arise from one standard
system?
\end{question}
Being elementary, analytic, and relatively algebraically closed is
necessary, but the oracle closure encoded by \cref{isg:ptg:prop:faithful}
imposes additional structure. The realization question should keep the
underlying Scott-set requirements separate from local field theory.

\begin{question}[Intrinsic reconstruction of computation]\label{isg:ptg:q:reconstruction}
Can Turing reducibility on $\mathcal S$ be reconstructed from the ring
$\Zhat(\mathcal S)$ with its named prime-coordinate residue maps, without
supplying an external oracle-coding convention?
\end{question}
\Cref{isg:ptg:thm:profinite,isg:ptg:cor:idempotents} answer the exact-image
and Boolean-reconstruction questions. They do not automatically recover the
additional computability relation on the idempotents. A useful next result
would specify the allowed language and distinguish definability,
interpretability, and external effective reconstruction.

\begin{question}[Regularity below analyticity]\label{isg:ptg:q:regularity}
Which regularity assumptions on $k$ suffice for the full perfect-gap
theorem when analyticity is unavailable?
\end{question}
A useful formulation should state regularity for the aggregate polynomial
relations and the graph of the induced derivation, not merely
measurability of $k$ itself. The transcendence-degree-one counterexample of
\cref{isg:ptg:rem:analytic} shows why unrestricted subfields cannot satisfy
the theorem.

\begin{question}[Beyond coefficient-preserving Hahn extensions]\label{isg:ptg:q:beyond}
Which transformations that mix coefficients and exponents preserve a
perfect scalar independence gap? What is the corresponding statement for
exponential or differential Hahn fields?
\end{question}
The coefficient proof answers the coefficient-preserving case exactly. An
exponential operation can convert coefficient information into an exponent
or vice versa, so a proposed extension must specify which operations and
which normal forms are available.

\begin{question}[Completions with a distinguished image]\label{isg:ptg:q:completions}
What additional invariant, beyond the abstract profinite completion,
classifies the pairs $(\widehat{(R_M)}_p,\im R_M)$ for Borel $\PA$ models,
and distinguishes them from omnific-type completion pairs?
\end{question}
\Cref{isg:ptg:thm:completions} proves that the distinguished image is
indispensable. Through the exact-image theorem it carries a standard-system
field, so computability and model-theoretic invariants have a direct entry
point. \textup{[}Added at this write: by \cref{isg:ptg:thm:residue} the distinguished image is
$\Zp(\SSy(M))$, so the pair determines the field $\mathcal K_p(\SSy(M))$,
and by \cref{isg:ptg:prop:faithful} the standard system, as a subset of $\Zp$; the profinite
version is \cref{isg:ptg:cor:images}.\textup{]}

\begin{question}[A machine-checked bridge]\label{isg:ptg:q:lean}
Can the Hahn independence theorem and the exact $p$-adic trace-image
theorem be verified in Lean with an explicit dependency ledger, without
adding the analytic corollaries as axioms?
\end{question}
The first target is coefficient algebra; the second requires correct
nonstandard arithmetic semantics. A useful initial contribution would prove
the finite recoding operation in every $\PA$ model, explicitly closing the
gap between standard-model formula evaluation and internal $\PA$
derivability identified in the source-04 paragraph of
\cref{isg:sec:audit}. \textup{[}Added at this write: for $\Oz$ the finite quotients and
completions are already Lean theorems (\cref{isg:ptg:sec:comparison}); the
repository has no Lean development of nonstandard models of $\PA$
(\cref{isg:ptg:sec:relationLean}).\textup{]}

\subsection{Non-claims, collected}\label{isg:sec:nonclaims}
The limitations stated at their places in the text, in the shipped
source audit and in the shipped provenance record of source~02 are
collected here, so that none is lost. Each stays in force where it was
stated. Items \textup{(N1)}--\textup{(N15)} are source~01's; in them
``the manuscript'' means source~01. Items \textup{(N16)}--\textup{(N29)}
are source~02's. Items \textup{(N30)}--\textup{(N41)} are source~03's,
with its shipped source audit, except \textup{(N41)}, which limits the
\textup{[write]} statements. Items \textup{(N42)}--\textup{(N56)} are
source~04's, with its shipped source audit and theorem ledger, except
\textup{(N56)}, which limits the \textup{[write~04]} statement.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(N\arabic*)},leftmargin=*]
\item \emph{Status.} The manuscript is unrefereed. It is a proposed
      solution with full proposed proofs, not an independently certified
      resolution; neither correctness nor novelty is certified, and no
      priority is claimed. The placement review
      (\cref{isg:sec:provenance}) is not a refereeing.
\item \emph{Literature.} The literature and repository searches were
      targeted, not exhaustive. A negative search result is limited
      evidence and does not exclude an unindexed paper, an unpublished
      proof or a reformulation known to specialists. That a question was
      posed in 2015 and 2018 does not establish that it was still
      unresolved on the review date.
\item \emph{Imported criterion.} \Cref{isg:fact:EK} is imported, not
      re-proved, by the manuscript or at placement. No proof here
      replaces it by the weaker assertion that initiality of the exponent
      class alone suffices.
\item \emph{Not claimed as new.} The two-level example and its map,
      the initial-Hahn-subgroup characterization, the classification of
      initial real groups (\cref{isg:fact:real}) and Conway normal forms
      are imported and cited. The extremal, quotient and surcomplex
      statements are derived refinements of the construction.
\item \emph{Not a simplicity isomorphism.} $\Rect$ is an ordered-group
      isomorphism onto an initial image. It need not preserve the
      simplicity relation (\cref{isg:rem:notsimplicity}); it is not
      multiplicative, does not preserve norms, and does not fix ordinary
      constants: $\Rect(1)\ne1$ in general (\cref{isg:sec:examples}).
\item \emph{No classification.} No classification of all ordered abelian
      groups admitting initial embeddings is claimed, and the
      initial-monoid problem (Question~3 of \cite{EK}, Question~9.2 of
      the journal version) is not addressed.
\item \emph{Exponent maps.} $\Theta_\alpha$ is not a homomorphism of
      exponent groups, and \eqref{isg:eq:leadingtransport} is not
      preservation of a multiplicative valuation
      (\cref{isg:rem:preserved}). \Cref{isg:prop:length} bounds exponent
      lengths, not the birthday of a transformed normal form.
\item \emph{No internal summation.} The strong-sum statements concern the
      ambient Hahn spaces; they give no internal summation on a subgroup
      $A$ (\cref{isg:rem:nocompletion}), and \cref{isg:lem:isolate} does
      not license arbitrary infinite subseries.
\item \emph{Invariance and uniqueness.} The ordinal $\alpha$ is not an
      invariant of the abstract ordered group. The uniqueness in
      \cref{isg:thm:B} is relative to the fixed map, not uniqueness of all
      initial embeddings, and the formulas are not claimed invariant under
      abstract isomorphisms.
\item \emph{Quotients.} It is not claimed that every convex quotient of
      an initial group has an initial realization. Iteration is covered
      only at finite stages (\cref{isg:cor:iteration}), with no claim at a
      limit stage, and \cref{isg:cor:relative} is a relative criterion
      that does not decide realizability of every quotient. (This is a
      statement about source~01. Source~02 proves both missing
      statements: \cref{isg:cf:main:factor} and
      \cref{isg:cf:cor:closure,isg:cf:cor:limit}. The relative criterion
      remains relative; \cref{isg:cf:main:presburger} decides the case of
      $\Z$-groups.)
\item \emph{Surcomplex scope.} \Cref{isg:thm:complex} is Gaussian-linear
      and coordinatewise initial. It gives no field isomorphism, no
      canonical sign-tree or simplicity relation on $\No[i]$, no
      preservation of the modulus, and no compatibility with
      multiplication by general surcomplex scalars. \Cref{isg:cor:linear}
      transports the right-hand side as well and does not apply to
      equations with ordinary constants held fixed, to nonlinear
      polynomials, to arbitrary surreal scalar matrices, or to norms and
      inner products.
\item \emph{Finite checks.} The finite checks do not verify transfinite
      concatenation or limit cases, arbitrary set-supported Hahn sums, the
      imported criterion, or the absence of a transfinite proof gap; the
      written proofs carry the general claims. Compiling and rendering the
      document establish its integrity, not its correctness.
\item \emph{No Lean.} No new Lean formalization and no Lean build are
      claimed. The proposed formalization units of \cref{isg:sec:audit} are
      not existing declarations, and a checked conditional theorem using
      the imported criterion would not by itself be a checked theorem
      about every initial subgroup of $\No$.
\item \emph{Repository review.} The manuscript's repository review was
      targeted: it did not review every report or archive, it treated
      truncated metadata as neither a complete inventory nor a proof of
      absence, it took no repository description as a certificate, and it
      promoted no repository result from pending to proved. The argument
      relies on no repository manuscript, and no repository Lean build was
      run for it.
\item \emph{Sources used only for context.} Bagayoko and van der Hoeven's
      sign-sequence conventions are context, not a source of the
      normalization or of its range theorem. The questions of this section
      are not asserted to be previously published open problems, and
      $d(H)=\On$ is a formal marker, not a surreal number or a set
      ordinal.
\item \emph{Status of source~02.} The convex-factor theorem and its
      applications are proposed contributions with complete proofs.
      Neither priority nor independent referee certification is claimed,
      no Lean formalization or Lean build is claimed, and the placement
      review (\cref{isg:sec:provenance}) is not a refereeing.
\item \emph{Credited, not new.} The Ehrlich--Kaplan criterion,
      Ehrlich's divisible embedding (\cref{isg:cf:fact:ehrlich}),
      Je\v r\'abek's residue theory and rigidity mechanism, and
      $\Ext(\Q,\Z)\cong\Zhat/\Z$ are classical and credited; no new
      computation of $\Ext$ is claimed, and the existence of rigid
      Presburger models is not claimed as a new phenomenon. The results
      of source~01 that source~02 uses (the suspension, the relative
      splitting criterion) are not claimed again as new.
\item \emph{No classification.} There is no classification of initially
      realizable groups. Convex splitting is necessary, and its
      sufficiency is not proved (\cref{isg:cf:sec:boundaries}).
\item \emph{Optimality question.} Source~02 does not resolve the
      Ehrlich--Kaplan set-model optimality question (Question~1 of
      \cite{EK}, Question~8.1 of the journal version) and gives no
      construction uniform over the intermediate theories
      (\cref{isg:cf:sec:optimality,isg:cf:rem:formulations}). It answers
      none of Ehrlich and Kaplan's questions.
\item \emph{Realization-dependent splitting.} The splitting of
      \cref{isg:cf:thm:split} depends on the chosen realization and is not
      invariant under abstract automorphisms; only the Presburger split
      case has the uniqueness of \cref{isg:cf:prop:residuesplit}.
\item \emph{Realizability, not literal initiality.} The results concern
      initial \emph{realizability}, not literal initiality of $C$ or of a
      complement in its original position.
\item \emph{No Hahn product, no completion.} An initial group is not
      identified with the full Hahn product of its factors, and no closure
      under ambient strong sums is asserted
      (\cref{isg:cf:rem:membership,isg:cf:rem:strongsums}).
\item \emph{Additive only.} The results are additive and
      module-theoretic. There is no multiplicative, field, ring,
      differential or exponential equivalence; the compression need not
      respect addition of exponents.
\item \emph{Surcomplex scope of source~02.} The rectangular notion is
      coordinate-dependent; no canonical sign tree on $\No[i]$ is
      postulated.
\item \emph{Finite checks of source~02.} The finite regression does not
      verify the Ehrlich--Kaplan theorem, arbitrary ordinals, class
      comprehension, completeness of Presburger arithmetic, recursive
      saturation or the Peano-model argument; the successful finite
      retractions are not a global splitting (\cref{isg:cf:rem:finite}).
      The delivered build and rendering checks concern presentation, not
      correctness.
\item \emph{Arithmetic fragment.} The fragment of arithmetic used for
      \cref{isg:cf:thm:PA} is not optimized. (This is a statement about
      source~02. With source~03, \cref{isg:sc:cor:fragment} reaches
      $\Idelta$ by a different argument; the fragment for the factorial
      type itself is still not optimized.)
\item \emph{Repository review of source~02.} Its review, at pin
      \texttt{9693b28}, was targeted: it was not a claim-by-claim audit
      of the repository, and no local Lean build was run.
\item \emph{Predecessor citation.} Source~02 cited source~01 without
      inventing a repository path for it; the current location
      (\cref{isg:sec:audit}) was supplied at this write.
\item \emph{Finite solvability.} Solvability of every finite retraction
      system is not a global splitting (\cref{isg:cf:rem:finite}).
\item \emph{Status of source~03.} It is an AI-assisted, unrefereed
      research draft. Its proofs are human-readable arguments, not a Lean
      or Rocq verification; no new Lean or Rocq proof is included or
      claimed, and the hand check of \cref{isg:sec:provenance} is not a
      refereeing.
\item \emph{Known phenomena credited.} Standard-cut definability and the
      failure of induction beyond open induction in $\Oz$ were already
      known (Je\v r\'abek 2011, Enayat--\L e\l yk--Visser) and are credited;
      bounded definability of $\N$ in $\Oz_{\ge0}$ is
      \cite[Theorem~2.9]{ELV}. The claimed contribution is the explicit
      dyadic refinement and its assembled consequences, not a certified
      solution of a previously open major conjecture. Global priority is
      not established for the exact formulations.
\item \emph{The correction is local.} The counterexample to
      \cite[Lemma~2.7]{ELV} does not claim that the paper's main theorems
      fail; Theorem~2.9 there needs only a chosen witnessing ideal. No full
      dependency audit of \cite{ELV} was performed, and it is not claimed
      that the error was previously unnoticed by its authors or by others.
\item \emph{Characters are not order-preserving.} A normalized integer
      character is not assumed to preserve order, and confusing it with a
      standard-part map would invalidate the arguments.
\item \emph{Unit condition.} The no-map theorems exclude unit-preserving
      additive maps; no theorem here excludes additive embeddings without
      the unit condition, which may land in the kernel of the target's
      character.
\item \emph{No regularity claimed.} The obstruction concerns the existence
      of maps, not their continuity, measurability, definability or
      computability; it is complementary to Glazer's topological theorem,
      not a strengthening of it, and answers neither of Glazer's questions.
\item \emph{Sharpness is about this detector.} The examples of
      \cref{isg:sc:prop:sharp} concern the dyadic formula only, not the
      existence of other definitions of the standard cut. No minimality
      of the quantifier pattern of $\StdDy$ is claimed.
\item \emph{Not a truth predicate.} The compiler produces a different
      formula for each input sentence; it is not a single uniform truth
      predicate taking syntax codes, and there is no conflict with Tarski's
      theorem. It needs an actually positive infinite bound; no computable
      test for infinitude is asserted; effective presentations are needed
      only for the upper reductions.
\item \emph{Imported results.} The open-induction property of
      $\Asc_{\mathrm{Sh}}$ is the classical integer-part theorem, consulted
      through \cite{Raffer}; Phillips's article is used only for the
      historical context of its publisher's extract, and no uninspected
      theorem of it is used.
\item \emph{Finite checks of source~03.} They are finite exact checks and
      syntactic audits, not machine-checked proofs of the universal
      theorems, and do not evaluate quantifiers at infinite bounds.
\item \emph{Repository review of source~03.} It consulted the reader's
      guides and \fn{OmnificResidues.lean} at its pin and is not a
      validation of every research claim in the repository. Elliot Glazer
      is not an author of source~03, and no review or endorsement by him is
      implied.
\item \emph{Scope of the \textup{[write]} statements.} They do not
      determine the weakest fragment asked for in \cref{isg:cf:q:fragment},
      do not show that $\Tdy$ is strictly weaker than $\Idelta$, and do not
      address whether nonstandard models of $\Idelta$ realize the
      factorial type $\tau_*$.
\item \emph{Status of source~04.} It is an AI-assisted, unrefereed research
      manuscript. Its proofs are ordinary mathematics; no Lean or Rocq
      verification of its new deductions is included or claimed, and the
      repository was not rebuilt for it. The hand check of
      \cref{isg:sec:provenance} is not a refereeing.
\item \emph{External inputs.} Glazer's standard-system theorem
      (\cref{isg:ptg:fact:glazer}), cited from seminar slides,
      Mycielski's theorem, classical descriptive set theory, quantifier
      elimination for real closed fields and for $\Qp$, and Edgar and
      Miller's dimension theorem are inputs, not new proofs. The $\EFA$
      and Borel-quotient strength of Glazer's theorem is not inherited by
      the new deductions, which use full Peano arithmetic.
\item \emph{Priority.} Historical priority is not established for the
      assembled theorem or for any component. Fatalini and Schindler's
      attribution to de Bondt and Glazer is a close precursor, credited;
      \cref{isg:ptg:lem:derivation} is near
      \cite[Proposition~2.6]{YeYuZhao}, and the relation was not compared
      at this write. Some ingredients may occur in the literature in
      another form; no ``breakthrough'' label is claimed.
\item \emph{Not Scott's problem, not Glazer's questions.} Neither the
      general Scott-set problem nor the reverse-mathematical questions of
      Glazer's topological paper is claimed resolved, and no arbitrary
      Turing ideal is claimed to be a standard system.
\item \emph{Topology of the omnific family.} The family of
      \cref{isg:ptg:thm:omnific} is Cantor-parameterized by its real
      coefficients but discrete in the surreal order topology and in the
      valuation topology (\cref{isg:ptg:sec:topologies}).
\item \emph{Restricted coefficient field.} Its independence is over
      $k_{\R,Y}((t^\Q))$, not over $\R$ or $\No$, and it is not
      independence of distinct valuation scales.
\item \emph{Set-sized only.} Only set-sized Hahn fields are used; $\No$ is
      never treated as a set, and no global well-order of the surreals is
      needed (\cref{isg:ptg:sec:classes}).
\item \emph{No effective witnesses.} The root lemma is nonuniform: no
      uniform all-roots selector and no algorithm for multiplicities. No
      member of an independent set is computable, no computable
      parametrization or injection of the Cantor sets exists, the
      continuous injections of \cref{isg:ptg:thm:simultaneous} need not be
      computable, and no single digit sequence is claimed to work at every
      place.
\item \emph{Measure and dimension.} No positive-measure independent Cantor
      set is claimed. Real Hausdorff dimension zero is inherited from Edgar
      and Miller, not a new theorem, and no $p$-adic dimension statement is
      made.
\item \emph{No embedding into $\Oz$.} Source~04 does not embed the Peano
      ring into $\Oz$; \cref{isg:ptg:cor:nomap} is a safeguard. What is
      transported is the scalar field of the standard system.
\item \emph{Scope of the countable advice.} \Cref{isg:ptg:cor:advice}
      concerns field generation and relative algebraic closure, not the
      enlargement of the Turing ideal by an oracle. The elementary subfields
      are not complete and come with no computable selector. No Polish
      topology on $R_M$, continuity of arithmetic, or Borel global root
      selection is assumed or claimed.
\item \emph{Questions.} The eleven questions are source~04's own, not
      established open problems.
\item \emph{Finite checks of source~04.} They check finite identities
      only, and certify no independence over an uncountable field, perfect
      set, Borel-model theorem, Glazer's theorem, overspill, standard system
      or transfinite Hahn arithmetic.
\item \emph{Repository review of source~04.} It read two repository
      guides at its pin through a connector, reviewed no report or formal
      file in full, and ran no build; it did not know this report or the
      independent-surreal-copies report, and its weaker or derivable
      statements are credited at their places. No third-party papers are
      redistributed with it. Elliot Glazer is not an author of source~04,
      and no review or endorsement by him is implied.
\item \emph{Scope of the \textup{[write~04]} statement.}
      \Cref{isg:ptg:cor:images} covers countable models of Peano arithmetic
      and rests on Scott's theorem, imported. It does not address
      uncountable models beyond the Scott-set description, or models of
      $\Idelta$.
\end{enumerate}
The statements marked \textup{[merge]} add no claim of priority; they are
proved from the two sources and, for \cref{isg:cf:cor:periods}, from
statements of the trigonometry report cited by label, whose own
non-claims remain in force there. The same holds for the statements marked
\textup{[write]}, proved from sources~02 and~03 and, for
\cref{isg:sc:rem:ELV}, from \cite[Theorem~2.9]{ELV}. The statement marked \textup{[write~04]} adds no claim of priority
either; it is proved from source~04 and Scott's theorem \cite{Scott}.

\section{Conclusion}\label{isg:sec:conclusion}
The obstruction in the Ehrlich--Kaplan question is not the numerical
size of the least positive element. Scalar multiplication removes that
size immediately. The obstruction is the interaction of the exponent
tree with coefficient groups forced by right ancestors.

The explicit map $\Theta_\alpha$ moves the minimum exponent to zero
and preserves all right-ancestor relations away from it. The coefficient
map then normalizes the discrete bottom group without disturbing the
higher groups. This gives the proposed affirmative proof. The relations
lost at the minimum are exactly those recorded by the dyadic spine,
which yields a necessary-and-sufficient image criterion rather than
only a sufficient construction.

The resulting theory is additive but strong: it transports arbitrary
set-supported normal forms without changing their support order types,
commutes with strong sums, identifies extremal initial groups at each
least positive scale, and produces initial models of the bottom cyclic
quotients and rectangular surcomplex modules. The remaining obligations
are independent mathematical review, a more exhaustive priority check,
and a complete formal verification of the identified dependency chain.

Source~02's compression construction makes convex-factor closure a
concrete normal-form theorem: retain an exponent interval, delete
precisely the ancestor positions outside it, and invoke the initial Hahn
criterion. It is coherent for nested intervals and works at arbitrary
transfinite cuts; for the bottom cyclic layer it is source~01's
$\rho\circ\tht$. At the same time, normal-form projection forces all
convex extensions inside an initial realization to split. In additive
arithmetic this yields a sharp boundary: a Presburger group has an
initial surreal realization exactly when each element's coherent
standard residues are ordinary integers. The explicit factorial family
places continuum many countable rigid models on the other side of that
boundary. Dense examples and omitted-type arguments show that the
distinction survives beyond discreteness and cannot be captured by
first-order theory alone. None of this is a certified resolution of the
full classification problem or of Ehrlich and Kaplan's optimality
question.

Source~03 shows that ordinary residue behaviour is not merely a benign
shadow of omnific arithmetic. An integer-valued character forces every
nonstandard element to have an explicit forbidden odd factor for the
dyadic predicate, turning a small bounded formula into an exact
standard-cut detector. Successor closure then converts that detector into
an induction obstruction and, after normalization of characters, a
prohibition on unit-preserving additive maps from nonstandard bounded
arithmetic. The same detector has the opposite effect on interpretation
power: inside any positive infinite interval it lets bounded formulas
recover all standard arithmetic truth, so a computable ring can have
true-arithmetic-complete bounded truth with a single common bound and
quadratic atoms. These are semantic and computability statements, not
claims of low-cost evaluation. Its polynomial counterexample separates
having a retraction onto $\Z$ from having a unique one, and division by
$2$ repairs the uniqueness. Read through source~02's criterion, the
additive group of a nonstandard model of bounded arithmetic has no initial
surreal realization, while open induction leaves room for one; the exact
threshold between them is open.

Source~04 reads the negative standard-system theorem positively, as a
source of large independent families. A Borel arithmetic model's
accessible local scalars form dense elementary subfields, but analytic
regularity prevents a finite or countably infinite transcendence basis from
closing the ambient local field. This yields conull and comeager
independent tuples and perfect independent sets, even after arbitrary
countable algebraic supplementation. The $p$-adic image of the Peano ring
is not merely bounded by that scalar field: it is exactly its valuation
ring. The full profinite image additionally recovers the standard system as
its Boolean algebra of idempotents and is strictly smaller than the product
of its local images when that system is proper; for Peano arithmetic this
answers the residue-image question of source~03, which stays open for
$\Idelta$. On the surreal side, coefficientwise Hahn arithmetic transports
the deficit without weakening it, and the resulting same-scale omnific
elements separate algebraic information from ordinary finite congruence
information. Identical completions can conceal different canonical images,
and Cantor coefficient structure can coexist with a discrete surreal order
topology.

\appendix
\section{A compact proof for independent checking}\label{isg:app:compact}

This appendix compresses the main existence argument without changing
its hypotheses. The full case analysis and support arguments are in the
body of the paper.

Let $A$ be a discrete initial subgroup. Its least positive member has a
single normal-form term: otherwise a nonzero tail in $A$ would be a
smaller positive element after taking absolute value. Write that term
as $c\omega^p$. The exponent class has minimum $p$, which, being the
minimum of an initial tree, is all-minus, say $p=-\alpha$.
The bottom real coefficient group is discrete and initial, hence
$c\Z$ with $c=2^{-n}$.

On $[p,\infty)$ define
\[
 p\mapsto0,\qquad
 p\cat\plus\cat u\mapsto\plus\cat p\cat u,\qquad
 x\mapsto\plus\cat x\quad(p\not\pre x).
\]
The inverse removes the initial plus and reinserts a plus immediately
after the block $p$ whenever the remaining word starts with $p$.
The two maps are increasing inverse bijections. For $x>p$,
\[
 \Pred(\Theta_\alpha x)
   =\Theta_\alpha[\Pred(x)\cup\{p\}],
 \qquad
 \Rs(\Theta_\alpha x)=\Theta_\alpha[\Rs(x)].
\]
Therefore an initial exponent class with minimum $p$ maps to an initial
one with minimum zero, and every new right-ancestor obligation comes
from an old one above $p$.

Transport each nonbottom term $r\omega^\gamma$ to
$r\omega^{\Theta_\alpha\gamma}$, and transport the bottom term
$r\omega^p$ to $r/c$. This is a real-linear order-isomorphism on the
ambient Hahn spaces. Its image of $A$ is truncation closed and
cross-sectional, has coefficient group $\Z$ at zero, and has unchanged
initial real coefficient groups at positive exponents. The right-ancestor
identity preserves all required dyadic containments. By the concrete
Ehrlich--Kaplan criterion, the image is initial. It lies in \Oz because
all exponents are nonnegative and the constant coefficient is integral.

For the inverse classification, only the ancestors of the old minimum
were discarded. They are $-\beta$ for $\beta<\alpha$, and their images
are $q_\beta=\plus\cat\minus^\beta$. Initial inversion requires
those exponents, while the right-ancestor condition at $p$ requires all
their dyadic coefficients. Together these requirements are exactly
$K_\alpha\subseteq H$.

\clearpage
\section{Proof-review checklist}\label{isg:app:checklist}
An independent review can focus on the following specific potential
failure points rather than checking experimental output as though it
were a proof.

\begin{longtable}{@{}p{0.30\textwidth}p{0.64\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
Issue & Location and required check\\
\midrule
\endhead
Concrete versus existential initiality &
\Cref{isg:fact:EK}: the imported sufficiency statement must apply to the
specified canonical image, not only to an unspecified isomorphic copy.\\[4pt]
Minimum exponent &
\Cref{isg:prop:bottom}: truncation and subtraction isolate a nonzero tail;
its absolute value is smaller than the positive leading monomial.\\[4pt]
Limit sign lengths &
\Cref{isg:lem:inverse,isg:thm:tree}: concatenated tails use ordinal interval
order types. No subtraction of ordinal lengths or removal of a last
limit sign is permitted.\\[4pt]
Exceptional old minimum &
\Cref{isg:thm:tree,isg:rem:exception}: the right-ancestor equality excludes
$x=p$. Its failure is used, not ignored, in the inverse classification.\\[4pt]
Coefficient group scaling &
\Cref{isg:lem:coefficients}: only the bottom coefficient is rescaled;
all right-ancestor obligations in the target are at unchanged groups.\\[4pt]
Infinite subgroup membership &
\Cref{isg:sec:transport}: transport the actual subgroup. Never replace it
with all normal forms allowed by its coefficient groups.\\[4pt]
Sharp inverse criterion &
\Cref{isg:thm:B}: inverse exponent initiality is not enough; the missing
minimum's ancestors must carry all dyadic coefficients.\\[4pt]
Quotient order &
\Cref{isg:lem:split,isg:prop:quotient}: higher nonzero terms dominate the cyclic
bottom group, and positive-root deletion supplies an initial image.\\[4pt]
Class-sized scope &
All supports and summation indices are sets; correspondences of
class-sized groups are expressed with class parameters.\\[4pt]
Surcomplex scope &
\Cref{isg:thm:complex}: the conclusion is Gaussian-linear and coordinatewise
initial, not a field isomorphism or a new canonical complex simplicity
relation.\\[4pt]
\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\emph{Source~02 (convex factors).}}\\[2pt]
Actual normal-form image &
\Cref{isg:fact:EK}: its sufficiency part must apply to the actual
transported image $\mathcal F_E(G)$, not just to an abstract copy.\\[4pt]
Restriction stays in $G$ &
\Cref{isg:cf:lem:restriction}: $P_E$ is the difference of two leading
truncations, so no infinite subseries is used.\\[4pt]
Meet retention &
\Cref{isg:cf:thm:compression}: compression must retain the sign at the
meet of an incomparable pair; convexity puts the meet in $E$
(\cref{isg:cf:lem:meetclosed,isg:cf:ex:badmeet}).\\[4pt]
Every target right ancestor &
\Cref{isg:cf:thm:compression,isg:cf:thm:transport}: every target right
ancestor must be accounted for, not merely every source ancestor; this is
the equality \eqref{isg:cf:eq:rightreflect}.\\[4pt]
Residue sign &
\Cref{isg:cf:prop:family}: the residue of $x_1$ is $-\zeta$, not
$\zeta$; factorial moduli are cofinal for divisibility.\\[4pt]
Divisible complement &
\Cref{isg:cf:prop:residuesplit}: a complement to $\Z e$ is divisible
because it is isomorphic to the quotient, not because every torsion-free
subgroup is divisible.\\[4pt]
Noninteger parameter &
\Cref{isg:cf:lem:noninteger}: $\zeta_*$ is shown to be neither a
nonnegative nor a negative ordinary integer.\\[4pt]
Internal sums &
\Cref{isg:cf:thm:PA}: the sum in a nonstandard Peano model is an
internally coded finite sum, not an external infinite sum in $G$.\\[4pt]
Merge statements &
\Cref{isg:cf:prop:cyclic,isg:cf:cor:limit,isg:cf:cor:periods}: the
identification with $\rho\circ\tht$, the one-point layers of the
iteration, and the order of $\mathcal I/\Z\cong\Pi_F$.\\[4pt]
\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\emph{Source~03 (standard cut) and the write.}}\\[2pt]
Ordinary-integer character &
\Cref{isg:sc:thm:dyadic}: $\chi$ takes values in the ordinary $\Z$; the
$2$-adic case split is external, and $m=\chi(y)$ may be zero or
negative.\\[4pt]
Positivity of witnesses &
\Cref{isg:sc:thm:dyadic,isg:sc:ex:certificates}: $u,v\ge0$ and the bounds
are checked in the ordered ring, not after applying $\chi$, which need not
preserve order.\\[4pt]
Unit condition &
\Cref{isg:sc:cor:noadd,isg:sc:cor:omnific}: only unit-preserving additive
maps are excluded.\\[4pt]
Infinite bound &
\Cref{isg:sc:thm:compiler,isg:sc:ex:bound}: the bound $\Omega$ must be
positive infinite; existential gate blocks stay inside each atomic
replacement.\\[4pt]
Version of the corrected lemma &
\Cref{isg:sc:prop:correction}: Lemma~2.7 of arXiv:2508.14758v2; the
repair needs $\ED_2$, which $\Z[X]$ lacks.\\[4pt]
The \textup{[write]} corollary &
\Cref{isg:sc:cor:fragment}: $G(M)$ is a set-sized $\Z$-group, so
\cref{isg:cf:main:presburger} applies; the argument uses no factorial
type.\\[4pt]
\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\emph{Source~04 (perfect transcendence gaps).}}\\[2pt]
Uncountable fields, countable parameters &
\Cref{isg:ptg:prop:hulls,isg:ptg:prop:relations}: category and measure are
applied to the aggregate relations $\Dep_n$, never to an enumeration of
polynomials over an uncountable $k$.\\[4pt]
Analytic graph &
\Cref{isg:ptg:lem:derivation}: the graph of $\widetilde\partial$ is
analytic; Borelness comes from totality and single-valuedness, continuity
from additivity.\\[4pt]
Internal sums and overspill &
\Cref{isg:ptg:thm:residue,isg:ptg:thm:profinite}: the sums are internally
coded finite sums at a nonstandard $N$; the coherent prefix comes from an
explicit overspill, not an external infinite sum.\\[4pt]
One shared oracle &
\Cref{isg:ptg:cor:idempotents}: $\Zhat(\mathcal S)$ requires one oracle for
all moduli and is strictly smaller than $\prod_p\Zp(\mathcal S)$.\\[4pt]
Coefficient field and topology &
\Cref{isg:ptg:thm:omnific}: independence over $k_{\R,Y}((t^\Q))$, not over
$\R$; the family is discrete in the surreal order topology.\\[4pt]
Strength of the imported theorem &
\Cref{isg:ptg:fact:glazer}: used only for nonstandard Borel models of
$\PA$; the trace lemmas use full induction.\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\section{The core convex-factor proof in one place}\label{isg:cf:app:core}
This appendix is source~02's. For independent checking, the logical chain
can be compressed without changing its hypotheses. Let $G\subseteq\No$ be
initial and let $C\subseteq C'$ be convex. The active exponents form an
initial class $\Gamma$. Convexity says that membership of a nonzero
element in $C$ or $C'$ is determined exactly by whether its leading
monomial belongs to that subgroup. Thus each has a lower exponent segment,
$\Gamma_C$ and $\Gamma_{C'}$.

The interval $E=\Gamma_{C'}\setminus\Gamma_C$ represents $C'/C$:
coefficient restriction to $E$ stays in $G$, because it is a difference
of two leading truncations. The kernel on $C'$ is $C$, and the
leading sign gives the quotient order.

Any two incomparable elements of $E$ have their common prefix between
them numerically. It is in $\Gamma$ by initiality and in $E$ by
convexity. Thus $E$ is meet closed. In each $\gamma\in E$, retain the
signs at precisely those positions $\beta$ for which
$\gamma\restriction\beta\in E$. Index them by their ordinal order
type. A source prefix in $E$ becomes a target prefix; incomparable
points keep opposite signs at their retained meet. Every target prefix
comes from one of the retained source prefixes. Hence the map is an
order-isomorphism onto an initial exponent class and preserves and
reflects right ancestry.

Reindex the normal forms supported in $E$ by this map. The coefficient
groups do not change, truncation closure and cross-sectionality persist,
and every target dyadic right-ancestor requirement is one of the
original requirements. The Ehrlich--Kaplan criterion makes this actual
image an initial subgroup of $\No$. This proves the convex-factor
assertion. Upper-exponent restriction simultaneously supplies the split
section for each convex subgroup sequence.

source.
